6. **Live truth** (`recon.py` output, code @ `4f7c294`) supersedes any static/aspirational claim — e.g. the best-practice guide's inline "✅ repo already does this" annotations are **aspirational and often false**; trust `recon.py` + code.
7. **Non-authoritative / do-not-build-from:** `docs/features/*`, `docs/swagger/careervp-api-v1.yaml` (drifted). The frontend contract oracle = `src/frontend/` code, not any spec doc.

### 0.3 Amendment process (immutability)
- No clause changes silently. Every change: bump `Version`, add a dated row to §12 Change Log, and update both MD and YAML in the same commit.
- Each clause is tagged `IMMUTABLE` (invariant — a violation is always a defect), `TARGET` (a state to reach), or `OPEN` (undecided — must be resolved before its dependent spec is written; never guessed).
- `current_state` vs `target` are recorded separately so partial implementation shows as partial, never as scope loss.

**Principle:** the contract is immutable to **drift** (silent, unversioned change), not to **learning**. Amendments are welcome; unrecorded changes are the defect. The contract changes at the *speed of decisions*, not the speed of code. Map = this contract; trail markers = specs; a washed-out trail → re-mark the trail (fix the spec), only re-draw the map when the destination or a hard constraint changed.

**Semver discipline:**
- **PATCH** (x.x.+1) — clarification / typo / `current_state` refresh. No scope change.
- **MINOR** (x.+1.0) — add a clause; refine a `TARGET`; resolve an `OPEN`.
- **MAJOR** (+1.0.0) — change an `IMMUTABLE` invariant or locked decision; drop a feature; break a frontend-contract item.

**Deviation → amendment loop (built into the spec/test/impl process — see execution-plan "Deviation & amendment handling"):** when a step's subagent cannot satisfy a clause as written, or live truth contradicts a `current_state`:
1. **Stop at the clause — never silently deviate.**
2. **Emit an Amendment Proposal:** `{clause_id, tag, what changed, why + evidence cite, semver level, affected specs/tests}`.
3. **Human validates and confirms** — no auto-apply.
4. **Update both twins in the same commit** + §12 change-log row + version bump.
5. **Propagate** to dependent specs/tests, then re-run `scope-diff.py`.
- **Adversarial review required** before committing any amendment to an `IMMUTABLE` invariant, locked decision, or frontend-contract item (§9.2).

**Contract self-protection (mechanical, not disciplinary — v2.0.0):** the two contract files (`project-scope-lock.md` + `.yaml`) are **write-protected from agent/orchestrator sessions** — an agent may *propose* an amendment but may not edit these files. **Amendments land only via a human-executed commit.** A CI check rejects any diff to either file that lacks: a §12 change-log row, a `Version` bump, twin-sync (both files changed together), and a human-signed approval trailer. *(Rationale: every downstream net — `scope-diff.py`, the oracle, wave gates — audits the code **against this contract**. If a session can silently edit the contract, `scope-diff.py` then enforces drift instead of preventing it — a silent total-loss of the "requirements can't rot" guarantee. The plan mechanized deploy discipline (P-27/P-28) after learning prompt-text guardrails don't bind agents; its own constitution gets the same treatment.)*

**Anti-patterns (hard-rejected — §9.3):** editing a *spec* to match the *code* instead of amending the contract when a decision changed · amending the contract *retroactively to rubber-stamp* what got built · weakening a test to pass. Amendments must cite evidence **and** a decision rationale, never "matches code."
=====§3=====
| L-7 | Priorities `T1/T2/T3`; effort `Lo/Md/Hi`. |

## 3. Frontend can't-break contract (IMMUTABLE) — `coverage-matrix.md §2`
The redesign is a data-layer + internal-identifier change that MUST keep API response shapes stable, **or version the route**. Internal PK is free to change; the wire contract is not.

1. `application_id == job_id` (one identifier, two names).
2. `artifact_id` is the round-tripped opaque key; a hub `artifact_id` MUST be resolvable by the status endpoint.
3. `vpr_id` = VPR's hub `artifact_id`; **CV-tailoring sends `vpr_id: null` (never omitted)** — null-vs-absent is load-bearing.
4. Status enum string-compared/unversioned (`pending|processing|completed|failed|cancelled|expired` + `not_generated`, `edited`); **changes must be additive.**
5. PATCH optimistic concurrency: echo `result.*` + `version` + `updated_at`; **HTTP 409 on stale `base_version`.**
6. `request_id` primacy (`request_id ?? job_id`).
7. Nested `result` shapes preserved (VPR/CV/IP/CL trees).
8. Presigned `download_url` + `status:'expired'` signal.
9. Existing shape polymorphism tolerated, must not worsen.
10. Error envelope (`error|message`, `classification`, `error_code`, `field`); 401 → one silent refresh-retry then sign-out.

**Oracle rule:** the authoritative contract = what `src/frontend/` actually calls + CDK `route_map` + handlers — NOT any OpenAPI/swagger doc (both are drifted).

**⚠️ Four live contract mismatches already exist** (real bugs — see F-02..F-05): VPR `download_url` missing from backend model; status enum lacks `cancelled/expired`; `vpr_id` required-non-null vs FE sends `null` (→422); error envelope nested vs FE expects flat (→`[object Object]`).

## 4. Architectural invariants (IMMUTABLE)
- Identity from validated JWT claims → `user_id` surrogate only; the DAL enforces the tenant partition key (IDOR structurally impossible); never re-fetch by a client-supplied `*_artifact_id`.
- A single **key-authority repository** is the sole key-builder; env-var table-precedence routing (`ARTIFACTS_TABLE_NAME → DYNAMODB_TABLE_NAME → TABLE_NAME`) is abolished; no new table alias.

| L-4 | **Billing live = launch-critical** (paid launch). |
| L-5 | **Company Research runs FIRST**, on new-application submit, **to completion — or a documented degraded fallback** — **then** gap analysis; CR is reused by all downstream artifacts. Requires reordering the artifact chain to `CR → gap → vpr → {cv, cover, interview}`. *(Failure semantics — refines the decision, does not reverse CR-first ordering/reuse: CR is a **soft-blocking** dependency. On CR `failed`/timeout > N seconds, gap proceeds degraded with an empty CR block — the prompt builder already renders nothing for an absent CR block (safe no-op) — and the application status surfaces CR's failure additively. Prevents a Tavily/CR-worker outage from stalling every submit behind a dead upstream.)* **Adversarial-review conditions (mandatory, close the C-2 + contract holes):** (a) **a degraded (empty-CR) gap MUST route to Haiku, never Sonnet** — L-6 pays Sonnet for the CR-fed quality lift, so running Strategic on empty inputs is the system's worst cost/quality trade and fires on CR's known reliability wart; gate Sonnet on a non-empty CR block. (b) `N` ships with a **concrete default** (proposed `N=180s`, matching the SFN heartbeat) — O-7 tunes it, it does not gate the constant's existence. (c) the "additive" status signal MUST name the exact enum value / degraded field and the F-01 oracle must confirm the frontend tolerates it (a fixed enum has no free room — "additive" is only proven for the prompt builder, not the status contract). **Semver: MAJOR (human-ruled 2026-07-11).** The adversarial refuter argued that relaxing "to completion" weakens a guarantee downstream code consumed; the human agreed and ruled this **MAJOR** — L-5's completion guarantee is **intentionally and explicitly relaxed** from "always to completion" to "to completion *or* documented degraded fallback (soft-blocking)". CR-first *ordering* and *reuse* remain locked; only the completion guarantee changed. This locked-decision change drove the v2.0.0 release. |

## 3. Frontend can't-break contract (IMMUTABLE) — `coverage-matrix.md §2`
The redesign is a data-layer + internal-identifier change that MUST keep API response shapes stable, **or version the route**. Internal PK is free to change; the wire contract is not.

1. `application_id == job_id` (one identifier, two names).
2. `artifact_id` is the round-tripped opaque key; a hub `artifact_id` MUST be resolvable by the status endpoint.
3. `vpr_id` = VPR's hub `artifact_id`; **CV-tailoring sends `vpr_id: null` (never omitted)** — null-vs-absent is load-bearing.
4. Status enum string-compared/unversioned (`pending|processing|completed|failed|cancelled|expired` + `not_generated`, `edited`); **changes must be additive.**
5. PATCH optimistic concurrency: echo `result.*` + `version` + `updated_at`; **HTTP 409 on stale `base_version`.**
6. `request_id` primacy (`request_id ?? job_id`).
7. Nested `result` shapes preserved (VPR/CV/IP/CL trees).
8. Presigned `download_url` + `status:'expired'` signal.
9. Existing shape polymorphism tolerated, must not worsen.
10. Error envelope (`error|message`, `classification`, `error_code`, `field`); 401 → one silent refresh-retry then sign-out.

**Oracle rule:** the authoritative contract = what `src/frontend/` actually calls + CDK `route_map` + handlers — NOT any OpenAPI/swagger doc (both are drifted).

**⚠️ Four live contract mismatches already exist** (real bugs — see F-02..F-05): VPR `download_url` missing from backend model; status enum lacks `cancelled/expired`; `vpr_id` required-non-null vs FE sends `null` (→422); error envelope nested vs FE expects flat (→`[object Object]`).

## 4. Architectural invariants (IMMUTABLE)
- Identity from validated JWT claims → `user_id` surrogate only; the DAL enforces the tenant partition key (IDOR structurally impossible); never re-fetch by a client-supplied `*_artifact_id`.
- A single **key-authority repository** is the sole key-builder; env-var table-precedence routing (`ARTIFACTS_TABLE_NAME → DYNAMODB_TABLE_NAME → TABLE_NAME`) is abolished; no new table alias.
- One typed home + one stored key per artifact type.
- Large bodies (VPR JSON, CV files) stay in **S3 with a pointer** (400KB / cost); "store rich, project lean" — storage shape ≠ prompt shape, both versioned.
- **Shared/cross-user data never lives in `core`** (partitioned by `USER#{user_id}`): Company Research cache (keyed by company), LLM cache, idempotency, **and the `sub→user_id` mapping (looked up *before* `user_id` is known, so it cannot be `USER#{user_id}`-keyed — its own small table or a `sub`-keyed GSI; see P-24)** stay separate tables.
- One IAM role per function, ARN-scoped, `{env}`-suffixed; no `Resource:"*"` where ARNs are known.
- Every SQS consumer reports `batchItemFailures`; visibility timeout **≥ 6× function timeout**; `max_concurrency` on rate-limited consumers; DLQ + depth alarm on every queue.

"""
Interview Prep Submit Handler for Async Architecture.

Endpoint: POST /interview-prep/generate
Flow:
  1. Validate InterviewPrepRequest
  2. Create PENDING artifact record in DynamoDB
  3. Send message to SQS queue
  4. Return 202 Accepted with job_id
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import datetime
import json
import os
import uuid
from http import HTTPStatus
from typing import Any

import boto3
from aws_lambda_powertools.utilities.typing import LambdaContext
from botocore.exceptions import ClientError as BotoClientError
from pydantic import ValidationError

from careervp.dal import table_registry
from careervp.dal.dynamo_dal_handler import DynamoDalHandler
from careervp.handlers.artifact_dependency_utils import (
    dependency_response_body,
    mark_requested_artifact_pending,
    resolve_handler_dependencies,
)
from careervp.handlers.auth_utils import extract_user_id
from careervp.handlers.cors_utils import get_cors_headers, set_request_origin
from careervp.handlers.utils.observability import logger, metrics, tracer
from careervp.logic.utils.constants import INTERVIEW_PREP_JOBS_QUEUE_NAME
from careervp.models.api_models import InterviewPrepRequest
from careervp.models.result import ResultCode

JSON_HEADERS = {'Content-Type': 'application/json'}


def _json_headers() -> dict[str, str]:
    return {'Content-Type': 'application/json', **get_cors_headers(None)}


# Module-level clients for testing/mocking
sqs = boto3.client('sqs')
dynamodb_resource = boto3.resource('dynamodb')


def _extract_authenticated_user_id(event: dict[str, Any]) -> str | None:
    return extract_user_id(event)


def _get_sqs_queue_url() -> str:
    """Resolve SQS queue URL deterministically from env or queue name lookup."""
    queue_url = os.environ.get('SQS_QUEUE_URL')
    if isinstance(queue_url, str) and queue_url.strip():
        return queue_url.strip()

    queue_name = str(os.environ.get('SQS_QUEUE_NAME', INTERVIEW_PREP_JOBS_QUEUE_NAME)).strip()
    if not queue_name:
        raise RuntimeError('SQS queue name is required')
    response = sqs.get_queue_url(QueueName=queue_name)
    resolved_url = response.get('QueueUrl')
    if not isinstance(resolved_url, str) or not resolved_url:
        raise RuntimeError(f'Unable to resolve SQS queue URL for queue {queue_name}')
    return resolved_url


def _get_artifacts_table_name() -> str:
    return table_registry.resolve_artifacts_table_name(required=True)


@logger.inject_lambda_context(log_event=False)
@tracer.capture_lambda_handler(capture_response=False)
@metrics.log_metrics(capture_cold_start_metric=True)
def lambda_handler(event: dict[str, Any], context: LambdaContext) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Handle POST /interview-prep/generate requests for async interview prep generation.

    Flow:
        1. Authenticate request
        2. Parse and validate request payload
        3. Create artifact record in DynamoDB with PENDING status
        4. Send message to SQS queue for async processing
        5. Return 202 Accepted with job_id

    Returns:
        202 Accepted: Job created successfully
        400 Bad Request: Invalid request payload
        401 Unauthorized: Missing authentication
        500 Internal Server Error: Infrastructure error
    """
    _ = context
    set_request_origin(event)
    endpoint = str(event.get('path', ''))
    tracer.put_annotation(key='endpoint', value=endpoint)
    logger.info(
        'Interview prep submit request received',
        api_gateway_event=event,
        endpoint=endpoint,
        request_id=_get_request_id(event, context),
    )
    authenticated_user_id = _extract_authenticated_user_id(event)
    if not authenticated_user_id:
        metrics.add_metric(name='UnauthorizedError', unit='Count', value=1)
        return _build_error_response(
            'Authentication required', HTTPStatus.UNAUTHORIZED, code=ResultCode.UNAUTHORIZED, request_id=_get_request_id(event, context)
        )

    tracer.put_annotation(key='user_id', value=authenticated_user_id)

    try:
        request_data = _parse_body(event)
        logger.info('Interview prep submit parsed request body', request_body=request_data)
        api_request = InterviewPrepRequest.model_validate(request_data)
        logger.info('Interview prep submit validated request body', validated_payload=api_request.model_dump(mode='json'))
    except ValidationError as exc:
        logger.warning('Invalid request body', error=str(exc))
        metrics.add_metric(name='ValidationError', unit='Count', value=1)
        return _build_validation_error_response(exc, event, context)
    except (ValueError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
        logger.warning('Invalid request body', error=str(exc))
        metrics.add_metric(name='ValidationError', unit='Count', value=1)
        return _build_error_response(
            'Invalid request body',
            HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST,
            code=ResultCode.INVALID_JSON,
            request_id=_get_request_id(event, context),
            validation_errors=[{'code': ResultCode.INVALID_JSON, 'field': 'body', 'message': str(exc)}],
        )

    resolved = _resolve_submit_preconditions(api_request, event, context)
    if isinstance(resolved, dict):
        return resolved
    table_name, application_id = resolved

    dependency_resolution = resolve_handler_dependencies(
        artifact_type='interview_prep',
        application_id=application_id,
        user_id=authenticated_user_id,
        dal=DynamoDalHandler(table_name),
    )
    if dependency_resolution.status != 'ready':
        if dependency_resolution.status == 'dependency_generating':
            mark_requested_artifact_pending(application_id=application_id, user_id=authenticated_user_id, artifact_type='interview_prep')
        return {
            'statusCode': dependency_resolution.http_status,
            'headers': _json_headers(),
            'body': json.dumps(dependency_response_body(dependency_resolution, requested_artifact='interview_prep')),
        }

    job_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
    artifact_id = table_registry.interview_prep_artifact_id(job_id)
    now = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)
    created_at = now.isoformat()

    logger.append_keys(user_id=authenticated_user_id, job_id=job_id)
    tracer.put_annotation(key='job_id', value=job_id)

    # Write PENDING artifact record to DynamoDB
    try:
        table = dynamodb_resource.Table(table_name)
        artifact_item = {
            **table_registry.legacy_item_key(authenticated_user_id, artifact_id),
            **table_registry.canonical_item_key(authenticated_user_id, artifact_id),
            'artifactType': 'interview_prep',
            'user_id': authenticated_user_id,
            'job_id': job_id,
            'status': 'PENDING',
            'request_data': api_request.model_dump(mode='json'),
            'created_at': created_at,
            'updated_at': created_at,
        }
        logger.info('Interview prep submit writing DynamoDB artifact', table_name=table_name, dynamodb_item=artifact_item)
        table.put_item(
            Item=artifact_item,
        )
    except BotoClientError as exc:
        logger.error('Failed to create artifact record', job_id=job_id, error=str(exc))
        metrics.add_metric(name='DynamoValidationException', unit='Count', value=1)
        return _build_error_response('Failed to create job', HTTPStatus.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR)

    # Send message to SQS
    try:
        queue_url = _get_sqs_queue_url()
        sqs_payload = {
            'job_id': job_id,
            'user_id': authenticated_user_id,
            'request_data': api_request.model_dump(mode='json'),
        }
        sqs_attributes = {
            'job_type': {'StringValue': 'interview_prep_generation', 'DataType': 'String'},
            'job_id': {'StringValue': job_id, 'DataType': 'String'},
            'user_id': {'StringValue': authenticated_user_id, 'DataType': 'String'},
        }
        logger.info(
            'Interview prep submit sending SQS message',
            queue_url=queue_url,
            sqs_message_body=sqs_payload,
            sqs_message_attributes=sqs_attributes,
        )
        sqs.send_message(
            QueueUrl=queue_url,
            MessageBody=json.dumps(sqs_payload),
            MessageAttributes=sqs_attributes,
        )
        logger.info('Interview prep job queued successfully', job_id=job_id, queue_url=queue_url)

    except BotoClientError as exc:
        logger.error('Failed to send message to SQS', job_id=job_id, error=str(exc))
        metrics.add_metric(name='SqsError', unit='Count', value=1)
        # Mark job as failed since we couldn't queue it
        try:
            table = dynamodb_resource.Table(_get_artifacts_table_name())
            table.update_item(
                Key={'applicationId': authenticated_user_id, 'artifactId': artifact_id},
                UpdateExpression='SET #s = :status, updated_at = :now',
                ExpressionAttributeNames={'#s': 'status'},
                ExpressionAttributeValues={':status': 'FAILED', ':now': now.isoformat()},
            )
        except BotoClientError:
            logger.error('Failed to mark job as failed', job_id=job_id)
        return _build_error_response('Failed to queue job for processing', HTTPStatus.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR)

    metrics.add_metric(name='InterviewPrepJobCreated', unit='Count', value=1)
    logger.info('Interview prep job created successfully', job_id=job_id)
    response_body = {
        'request_id': job_id,
        'artifact_id': job_id,
        'status': 'processing',
        'estimated_time_seconds': 60,
    }
    logger.info('Interview prep submit response payload', response_status_code=int(HTTPStatus.ACCEPTED), response_body=response_body)

    return {
        'statusCode': int(HTTPStatus.ACCEPTED),
        'headers': _json_headers(),
        'body': json.dumps(response_body),
    }


def _parse_body(event: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Parse the API Gateway event body into a dictionary."""
    body = event.get('body')
    if body is None:
        raise ValueError('Request body is required.')

    if isinstance(body, dict):
        return body

    if isinstance(body, (bytes, bytearray)):
        decoded = body.decode('utf-8')
        parsed = json.loads(decoded)
    elif isinstance(body, str):
        parsed = json.loads(body)
    else:
        raise ValueError('Unsupported body type.')

    if not isinstance(parsed, dict):
        raise ValueError('Request body must be a JSON object.')
    return parsed


def _resolve_submit_preconditions(
    api_request: InterviewPrepRequest,
    event: dict[str, Any],
    context: LambdaContext,
) -> tuple[str, str] | dict[str, Any]:
    """Resolve `(artifacts_table_name, application_id)`, or return an error response.

    Both preconditions live here so `lambda_handler` spends one decision point on
    them instead of two — it is already at the C901 complexity ceiling.
    """
    try:
        table_name = _get_artifacts_table_name()
    except RuntimeError:
        logger.exception('Artifacts table configuration error')
        metrics.add_metric(name='MissingEnvError', unit='Count', value=1)
        return _build_error_response(
            'Internal server error', HTTPStatus.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, code=ResultCode.MISSING_ENV, request_id=_get_request_id(event, context)
        )

    # v3.0.0 / scope-lock A1 (P-01): ONE canonical application key. A client-supplied
    # vpr_id may never stand in for it — that is legacy-id resolution, which O-3 forbids.
    # Refuse BEFORE dependency resolution so an identity-less request performs no
    # resolver read and no mark_requested_artifact_pending write into the canonical hub.
    application_id = api_request.application_id or api_request.job_id
    if not application_id:
        logger.warning('Interview prep submit rejected: no application identity')
        metrics.add_metric(name='MissingApplicationIdentity', unit='Count', value=1)
        # request_id is deliberately NOT passed here: AC-P01-1 pins this exact
        # three-key envelope. Adding it would break the pinned contract.
        return _build_error_response(
            'application_id/job_id is required',
            HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST,
            code=ResultCode.MISSING_REQUIRED_FIELD,
        )

    return table_name, application_id


def _build_error_response(
    message: str,
    status: HTTPStatus,
    code: str | None = None,
    request_id: str | None = None,
    validation_errors: list[dict[str, str]] | None = None,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Construct a standardized error response."""
    payload: dict[str, Any] = {
        'error': message,
        'status_code': int(status),
    }
    if code:
        payload['code'] = code
    if request_id:
        payload['request_id'] = request_id
    if validation_errors is not None:
        payload['validation_errors'] = validation_errors
    return {
        'statusCode': int(status),
        'headers': _json_headers(),
        'body': json.dumps(payload),
    }


def _validation_errors(exc: ValidationError) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
    errors: list[dict[str, str]] = []
    for err in exc.errors():
        location = err.get('loc', ())
        field = '.'.join(str(part) for part in location) if isinstance(location, tuple) else str(location)
        errors.append(
            {
                'code': ResultCode.VALIDATION_ERROR,
                'field': field,
                'message': str(err.get('msg', 'Invalid value')),
            }
        )
    return errors


def _get_request_id(event: dict[str, Any], context: LambdaContext | None) -> str | None:
    request_context = event.get('requestContext')
    if isinstance(request_context, dict):
        request_id = request_context.get('requestId')
        if isinstance(request_id, str) and request_id:
            return request_id
    if context is not None:
        request_id = getattr(context, 'aws_request_id', None)
        if isinstance(request_id, str) and request_id:
            return request_id
    return None


def _build_validation_error_response(
    exc: ValidationError,
    event: dict[str, Any],
    context: LambdaContext,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return _build_error_response(
        message='Invalid request body',
        status=HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST,
        code=ResultCode.VALIDATION_ERROR,
        request_id=_get_request_id(event, context),
        validation_errors=_validation_errors(exc),
    )

def build_schema_bundle() -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
    return {schema_name: model.model_json_schema(ref_template='#/$defs/{model}') for schema_name, model in sorted(CONTRACT_MODELS.items())}


def emit_schema_files(output_dir: Path = DEFAULT_OUTPUT_DIR) -> list[Path]:
    output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    written: list[Path] = []

def collect_clause_ids(yaml_path: Path) -> list[str]:
    """Return all backlog clause IDs from the scope-lock YAML."""
    with open(yaml_path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        doc = yaml.safe_load(f)
    return [entry["id"] for entry in doc.get("backlog", [])]


def collect_specs(specs_dir: Path) -> dict[str, dict]:
    """
    Return {clause_id: {file, spec_id, status, contract_impact, tooling_ok}} for every spec.
    Handles both single and list-valued scope_lock_clause.
    """
    result: dict[str, dict] = {}
    if not specs_dir.exists():
        return result
    for md in specs_dir.glob("*.md"):
        fm = parse_frontmatter(md)
        clause_field = fm.get("scope_lock_clause")
        if clause_field is None:
            continue
        clauses = (
            [clause_field] if isinstance(clause_field, str) else list(clause_field)
        )
        for clause_id in clauses:
            # For multi-clause specs, check tooling entry exists
            tooling_ok = True
            if isinstance(fm.get("scope_lock_clause"), list):
                tooling = fm.get("tooling", {})
                tooling_ok = clause_id in tooling
            result[str(clause_id)] = {
                "file": md.name,
                "spec_id": fm.get("spec_id", ""),
                "status": fm.get("status", "draft"),
                "contract_impact": fm.get("contract_impact", False),
                "tooling_ok": tooling_ok,
                "multi_clause": isinstance(fm.get("scope_lock_clause"), list),
            }
    return result


def collect_test_references(*tests_dirs: Path) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
    """
    Scan all .py files under each of tests_dirs for clause ID references.
    Returns {clause_id: [file_path, ...]}
    """
    result: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    # Match patterns like scope_lock_clause='P-27', # P-27, "Q-02", etc.
    clause_pattern = re.compile(r"\b([PDQTFX]-\d+[a-z]?)\b")
    for tests_dir in tests_dirs:
        if not tests_dir.exists():
            continue
        for py in tests_dir.rglob("*.py"):
            try:
                text = py.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
            except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
                continue
            for m in clause_pattern.finditer(text):
                cid = m.group(1)
                result.setdefault(cid, [])
                rel = str(py)
                if rel not in result[cid]:
                    result[cid].append(rel)
    return result


def determine_impl_state(
    clause_id: str,
    spec_map: dict[str, dict],
    test_map: dict[str, list[str]],
    impl_states_from_yaml: dict[str, str],
) -> str:
    has_spec = clause_id in spec_map
    has_test = clause_id in test_map and len(test_map[clause_id]) > 0
    yaml_state = impl_states_from_yaml.get(clause_id)
    if yaml_state in ("implemented", "verified"):
        return yaml_state
    if has_test:
        return "test_written"
    if has_spec:
        return "spec_written"
    return "not_started"


def load_yaml_impl_states(yaml_path: Path) -> dict[str, str]:
    with open(yaml_path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        doc = yaml.safe_load(f)
    states: dict[str, str] = {}
    for entry in doc.get("backlog", []):
        if "impl_state" in entry:
            states[entry["id"]] = entry["impl_state"]
    return states

    # v3.0.0 / scope-lock A1 (P-01): ONE canonical application key. A client-supplied
    # vpr_id may never stand in for it — that is legacy-id resolution, which O-3 forbids.
    # Refuse BEFORE dependency resolution so an identity-less request performs no
    # resolver read and no mark_requested_artifact_pending write into the canonical hub.
    application_id = api_request.application_id or api_request.job_id
    if not application_id:
        logger.warning('Interview prep submit rejected: no application identity')
        metrics.add_metric(name='MissingApplicationIdentity', unit='Count', value=1)
        # request_id is deliberately NOT passed here: AC-P01-1 pins this exact
        # three-key envelope. Adding it would break the pinned contract.
        return _build_error_response(
            'application_id/job_id is required',
            HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST,
            code=ResultCode.MISSING_REQUIRED_FIELD,
        )
_build_error_response (same file, lines 305–327) builds {'error': message, 'status_code': int(status)} and adds 'code' only if passed, 'request_id' only if passed. With ResultCode.MISSING_REQUIRED_FIELD = 'MISSING_REQUIRED_FIELD' (src/backend/careervp/models/result.py:100), the body is exactly {"error": "application_id/job_id is required", "status_code": 400, "code": "MISSING_REQUIRED_FIELD"}. The AC-P01-1 comment exists verbatim at lines 294–295.

Claim 2 — ErrorResponse.json is additionalProperties: false over exactly {error, message, classification, error_code, field}; 400 body fails validation
VERDICT: VERIFIED.

/Users/yitzchak.meirovich/Documents/code5/careervp/src/backend/contract/schemas/ErrorResponse.json — line 2: "additionalProperties": false; properties are exactly classification, error, error_code, field, message, each anyOf [string, null], default null.

Live validation (uv run --no-project --with jsonschema python, Draft 2020-12 validator, from src/backend):


INVALID
- Additional properties are not allowed ('code', 'status_code' were unexpected)
The 400 body fails on both extra keys (code, status_code); it has no missing-key failure since all five schema properties are optional.

Claim 3 — zod .strict() five-key schema; client reads payload?.error_code so code is lost
VERDICT: VERIFIED (nuance: .strict() means a code key would cause a zod parse rejection, not silent stripping; and independently the axios interceptor never reads code).

/Users/yitzchak.meirovich/Documents/code5/careervp/src/frontend/lib/contractSchemas.ts:274-285:


export const flatErrorEnvelopeSchema = z
  .object({
    error: z.string().optional(),
    message: z.string().optional(),
    classification: z.string().optional(),
    error_code: z.string().optional(),
    field: z.string().optional(),
  })
  .strict()
  .refine((value) => value.error !== undefined || value.message !== undefined, {
    message: 'flat error envelope requires error or message',
  });
(It is registered as ErrorResponse in contractSchemas at line 294.)

/Users/yitzchak.meirovich/Documents/code5/careervp/src/frontend/api/client.ts:100-107:


    const status = error.response?.status ?? 0;
    const payload = error.response?.data;
    const message = payload?.error ?? payload?.message ?? error.message;
    throw new ApiError(status, message, {
      classification: payload?.classification,
      errorCode: payload?.error_code,     // line 105
      field: payload?.field,
    });
So at runtime the FE would surface the error string as the message but ApiError.errorCode would be undefined — the backend's code key is never read. Under the F-01 zod oracle, the body is rejected outright (status_code/code unrecognized keys under .strict()).

Claim 4 — Envelope builder predates the amendment; the handler was already non-conformant on other paths
VERDICT: VERIFIED (with one precision: the exact three-key variant {error,status_code,code} with no request_id is new with a485293; the {error,status_code,...} family — which is what violates item 10 — predates the amendment on every error path of this handler).

Builder ancestry — git log -S '_build_error_response' -- src/backend/careervp/handlers/interview_prep_submit_handler.py:


a485293 fix(p01): A1 — remove vpr_id application-key fallback on interview-prep submit

6afc671, 652f959, 413899f are all dated 2026-03-02; the four amendment commits (0ef8971, ae18e30, a485293 — all 2026-07-27; 7807e43 docs) are ~5 months later. The builder predates the amendment by roughly five months.

Every error emission in the pre-amendment file (git show a485293^:src/backend/careervp/handlers/interview_prep_submit_handler.py), with resulting body keys:

Old path	Keys emitted
401 'Authentication required' (old L109-111)	{error, status_code, code, request_id}
Pydantic ValidationError → _build_validation_error_response (old L118/L363-371)	{error, status_code, code, request_id, validation_errors}

Crucially, the old file had no missing-identity refusal at all — old L143 read application_id = api_request.application_id or api_request.job_id or api_request.vpr_id, so vpr_id silently filled the gap and execution proceeded.

Item-10 conformance before the amendment: zero of these paths conform. All include status_code (rejected by additionalProperties:false), none emits error_code/classification/field at top level (the closest is field nested inside validation_errors, itself an unrecognized key). So "A1 broke item 10" is not fair as stated: the amendment added one more path to an already-non-conforming family this same handler used on every pre-existing refusal. What IS new: the exact three-key {error,status_code,code} combination (code present, request_id absent) did not exist in this handler before a485293 — prior code-bearing paths all carried request_id, and the two-key 500s carried neither. Today's other emissions (current file lines 109, 127, 184, 226, 282, 363) still use the same non-conforming family; only line 296 is the pinned three-key variant.

Claim 8 — Does §3/item 10 cover a NEW error response on an existing route?
VERDICT: PARTIALLY — plausible on the text, contestable on the preamble's framing. (Judgment call; evidence both ways.)

Preamble, project-scope-lock.md:90-91:


## 3. Frontend can't-break contract (IMMUTABLE) — `coverage-matrix.md §2`
The redesign is a data-layer + internal-identifier change that MUST keep API response shapes stable, **or version the route**. Internal PK is free to change; the wire contract is not.
Item 10, project-scope-lock.md:102:


10. Error envelope (`error|message`, `classification`, `error_code`, `field`); 401 → one silent refresh-retry then sign-out.
For coverage (item 10 applies to the new 400): Item 10 is written as an unconditional shape rule for "Error envelope" — no scoping to existing paths; a 400 body on an unversioned existing route is an "API response shape" the frontend will parse (client.ts's interceptor runs on every non-2xx, including brand-new ones, reading error_code/classification/field); and F-05's crosswalk "contract#10" shows the lock treats any envelope non-conformance as an item-10 matter. The route was not versioned, which is the preamble's only escape hatch.

Against coverage (or against MAJOR resting on it): the preamble scopes §3 to keeping shapes stable — a guarantee about not changing what the frontend already consumes; a request that previously proceeded (via vpr_id fallback) now refusing is a behavior change (already priced into the MAJOR classification), and its envelope shape breaks nothing that previously worked, since (per claim 4) every pre-existing refusal on this same route already emitted the non-conforming {error,status_code,...} family — the frontend could not read error_code from this handler before the amendment either. Item 9 ("Existing shape polymorphism tolerated, must not worsen") cuts both ways: one can argue adding another non-conforming envelope instance "does not worsen" existing polymorphism, or that pinning it via exact-equality RED test (claim 5) entrenches and thus worsens it. Also of note (claim 6): the same amendment's 403 envelope IS conformant, undermining any "the handler's local convention required three keys" defense.

Net: the round-1 rationale ("the new 400 violates item 10") is textually defensible, but the sharper framing is that the amendment pinned (via AC-P01-1 exact-equality test and an explicit do-not-add-request_id comment) a pre-existing item-10 non-conformance on a new refusal path, rather than breaking a previously conformant surface.

28-### 0.3 Amendment process (immutability)
29-- No clause changes silently. Every change: bump `Version`, add a dated row to §12 Change Log, and update both MD and YAML in the same commit.
30-- Each clause is tagged `IMMUTABLE` (invariant — a violation is always a defect), `TARGET` (a state to reach), or `OPEN` (undecided — must be resolved before its dependent spec is written; never guessed).
31-- `current_state` vs `target` are recorded separately so partial implementation shows as partial, never as scope loss.

rule 4 (check your own prerequisites), applied specifically to the spec/test ordering, and it is
why a skeleton is not itself sufficient to start writing tests.

7. QUOTE HUNT — PARTIALLY VERIFIED: the phrase exists, but NOT in the amendment proposal or the scope-lock amendment
The exact phrase "an either-or rule is not a per-field constraint" appears in exactly two places in the paper trail:

src/backend/careervp/models/api_models.py:505-507 (code comment): "Both stay typed optional because an either-or rule is not a per-field constraint; it is enforced at the handler boundary, which owns the pinned 400 envelope."
Commit ae18e30 message (the contract+tests commit): "Fields stay typed optional because an either-or rule is not a per-field constraint — it is enforced at the handler, which owns the pinned 400 envelope."
It does NOT appear in: the amendment proposal specs/amendments/D-H4-P-01-submit-test-conflict-amendment.md (read in full — no hit for per-field/either-or/either/or/model_validator/one-of), either scope-lock twin's v3.0.0 change-log row, the spec D-H4-P-01-canonical-artifact-spec.md, or commits 0ef8971/7807e43/a485293.

Crucially, the amendment paper trail never discusses or rejects a Pydantic model_validator (either-or model-layer check) at all. The only model-layer alternative it rejects is a different mechanism: the proposal's "Rejected workaround" (:100-107) — "Do not branch on Pydantic model_fields_set to accept omitted identifiers while rejecting explicit null" (= option A3 in the change-log), rejected because it would preserve the vpr_id fallback, not because either-or checks don't belong in models. So: if Round 1 attributed the "either-or is not a per-field constraint" rationale to "the amendment" (proposal or v3.0.0 twin change), that attribution is wrong — the rationale exists only in the implementation-side code comment and commit ae18e30's message, and no document anywhere weighs a model_validator alternative. (model_validator is used elsewhere in the repo, e.g. careervp/models/cv.py:73, so the pattern is available.)

8. Current InterviewPrepRequest — quoted
src/backend/careervp/models/api_models.py:499-519:


class InterviewPrepRequest(APIModel):
    vpr_id: str = Field(min_length=1)
    gap_response_ids: list[str]
    focus_areas: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)
    question_count: int = 10
    # Application identity (section 3.7). v3.0.0 / scope-lock decision A1: at least ONE
    # of application_id / job_id is REQUIRED. Both stay typed optional because an
    # either-or rule is not a per-field constraint; it is enforced at the handler
    # boundary, which owns the pinned 400 envelope. The former promise that "clients
    # that omit them continue to work" is WITHDRAWN — a client-supplied vpr_id no
    # longer stands in for the canonical application key (O-3: no legacy-id resolution).
    application_id: str | None = None
    job_id: str | None = None
    language: str = 'en'

    @field_validator('gap_response_ids')
    @classmethod
    def _gap_ids_required(cls, value: list[str]) -> list[str]:
        if not value:
            raise ValueError('gap_response_ids must not be empty')
        return value
Answers: yes, vpr_id is still accepted — and still REQUIRED (min_length=1); application_id/job_id remain Optional (str | None = None) at the model layer, enforcement handler-only; the comment says exactly the above (lines 504-509).

9. Handler behavior for a vpr_id-only request — traced (whole file read)
src/backend/careervp/handlers/interview_prep_submit_handler.py:

A body with ONLY vpr_id (plus non-empty gap_response_ids) passes Pydantic at :118. Then :135 calls _resolve_submit_preconditions, where :290 application_id = api_request.application_id or api_request.job_id yields None → :296-300 returns HTTP 400, message 'application_id/job_id is required', code=ResultCode.MISSING_REQUIRED_FIELD — before resolve_handler_dependencies (:140), before mark_requested_artifact_pending (:148), and before the DynamoDB put_item (:178). No resolver read, no write. Matches the P-01 v3.0.0 clause text exactly.
Is a supplied vpr_id used anywhere in the submit path? Never as an identifier. Its only appearances in the flow are inside api_request.model_dump(mode='json'), embedded whole as request_data in the PENDING artifact item (:173) and in the SQS payload (:192) — i.e., for an identity-carrying request it is forwarded opaquely to the async worker as payload; the submit handler itself never reads api_request.vpr_id. So as an application key it is ignored (and an identity-less request is refused, not silently processed), yet paradoxically the model still makes vpr_id mandatory (min_length=1) — a request omitting it fails 400 VALIDATION_ERROR at :118-123.
Bottom line
Round 1's central textual claim is CORRECT: O-3's resolved decision (YAML :257) is exclusively about stored-data lifecycle and contains no legacy-id-resolution norm; the MD/YAML twins desynced at commit 9260f37; and the "O-3 forbids legacy-id resolution" gloss is hard-coded in at least 9 locations (more than Round 1's five). D-H9 (md:173) + §3 item 1 (md:93) are the textually apt authorities. One Round 1 attribution error found (item 7): the "either-or rule is not a per-field constraint" rationale is NOT in the amendment proposal or the v3.0.0 scope-lock change — it exists only in the api_models.py:506 comment and commit ae18e30's message, and no document in the paper trail ever considers a model_validator alternative.

The §0.3 proposal inventoried ONE affected test; the real count is eight. The other seven were found only by running the full suite while landing A1. All seven are the same class — fixtures that omitted application_id and silently relied on the vpr_id application-key fallback — so they fall inside the same approved reconciliation rather than needing their own. = exactly 8 named tests. Adjudication:

Round 1's 2+5=7 is correct for the two A1 commits (ae18e30 + a485293). The eighth named test is the B1 CV-tailoring fixture changed in 0ef8971, which round 1's arithmetic excluded because it only diffed the A1 commits. So "the real count is eight" is right as the inventory-wide count, and round 1 missed the B1 row.
However, the spec's own framing sentence is internally inconsistent by one: "the other seven were found only by running the full suite while landing A1. All seven are the same class — fixtures that omitted application_id…" — only six tests fit that description (marks_failed + 5 roundtrip). The eighth test (CV-tailoring) is a different class (omitted vpr_id, not application_id) and was found via the documented conflict amendment, not the full-suite run. The "= 8 total" line's addition (1+5+1 "and its sibling above") also double-counts marks_failed as written. The total of 8 is defensible; the "other seven / same class" sentence is not strictly accurate. The a485293 commit message ("reconciles seven further affected fixtures") has the same off-by-one looseness — that commit itself changed only the helper feeding 5 tests.

def _resolve_cover_letter_dependency_response(
    *,
    api_request: CoverLetterRequest,
    user_id: str,
    table_name: str,
) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
    application_id = api_request.application_id or api_request.job_id
    dependency_resolution = resolve_handler_dependencies(
        artifact_type='cover_letter',
        application_id=application_id,
        user_id=user_id,
        dal=DynamoDalHandler(table_name),
    )
    if dependency_resolution.status == 'ready':
        return None
    if dependency_resolution.status == 'dependency_generating':
        mark_requested_artifact_pending(application_id=application_id, user_id=user_id, artifact_type='cover_letter')
    return {
        'statusCode': dependency_resolution.http_status,
        'headers': _json_headers(),
        'body': json.dumps(dependency_response_body(dependency_resolution, requested_artifact='cover_letter')),
    }


def _parse_body(event: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Parse the API Gateway event body into a dictionary."""
    body = event.get('body')
    if body is None:
        raise ValueError('Request body is required.')

    if isinstance(body, dict):
        return body

    if isinstance(body, (bytes, bytearray)):
        decoded = body.decode('utf-8')
        parsed = json.loads(decoded)
    elif isinstance(body, str):
        parsed = json.loads(body)
    else:
        raise ValueError('Unsupported body type.')

    if not isinstance(parsed, dict):
        raise ValueError('Request body must be a JSON object.')
    return parsed


def _build_error_response(
    message: str,
    status: HTTPStatus,
    code: str | None = None,

(1) Specific verifications A–G
A. Handler ordering + guard (VERIFIED, with edge cases)
Read /Users/yitzchak.meirovich/Documents/code5/careervp/src/backend/careervp/handlers/interview_prep_submit_handler.py completely (370 lines). Order: auth (:106-111, 401 first) → body parse/pydantic (:115-133) → _resolve_submit_preconditions (:135) → dependency resolution (:140) → pending put_item (:178). The guard (:290-300):


application_id = api_request.application_id or api_request.job_id
if not application_id:
    logger.warning('Interview prep submit rejected: no application identity')
    metrics.add_metric(name='MissingApplicationIdentity', unit='Count', value=1)
    # request_id is deliberately NOT passed here: AC-P01-1 pins this exact
    # three-key envelope. Adding it would break the pinned contract.
    return _build_error_response('application_id/job_id is required', HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST, code=ResultCode.MISSING_REQUIRED_FIELD)
The 400 fires before any resolver read and before any write — empirically proven by running the real handler (probe script in scratchpad): resolver_called=False on the refusal path, and mark_requested_artifact_pending is only reachable after resolution. One ordering nuance: _get_artifacts_table_name() runs before the identity check (:277-284), so a misconfigured env returns 500 MISSING_ENV even for identity-less requests — env resolution, not a data read; acceptable but unpinned.

Edge cases (all run against the real handler):

application_id: "", job_id: "" → 400 MISSING_REQUIRED_FIELD despite fields being "sent". Contract-coherent enough (empty = missing), but nothing pins it.
Whitespace-only " " → also 400 — saved not by the guard but by APIModel's str_strip_whitespace=True (api_models.py:16). Coherent, but the protection lives in a base-model config the amendment never mentions.
application_id="", job_id="job-B" → 202-path, resolver receives 'job-B'. Alias works.
application_id='app-A', job_id='job-B' (mismatch) → silently resolves to 'app-A'; no equality check. See fresh finding 4.
Non-string (123) → pydantic 400 VALIDATION_ERROR with request_id + validation_errors — the other envelope, not the pinned three-key one. Coherent (different failure class).
B. Frontend types (VERIFIED, one incoherence)

(2) Fresh findings, ranked
1. HIGH — The worker (the real production path behind every 202) still routes VPR reads by the client-supplied vpr_id; submit-gate and worker use different keys.
interview_prep_handler.py:239-242: the SQS worker calls _generate_interview_prep_result(api_request=..., user_id=...) passing neither application_id nor vpr_artifact_id. So in _resolve_interview_prep_context, :849 resolved_artifact_id = vpr_artifact_id or api_request.vpr_id — the raw client key from the queued request_data — drives _resolve_vpr_from_jobs_table(..., artifact_id=<client vpr_id>) and get_vpr_by_artifact_id(...). Meanwhile the submit handler's dependency gate checked VPR existence by application_id (artifact_dependency_utils.py: dal.get_vpr(application_id=application_id)). Consequence: a request with a valid application_id but stale/wrong vpr_id gets a 202 "processing" (gate passes on application_id) and then a guaranteed async FAILED (:879 raise ValueError('Required VPR not found...')) because the worker keys on the client value the gate never validated. AC-P01-1 ("resolved owned upstreams and not arbitrary client keys") is enforced only in the sync handler's _resolve_request_vpr_identity; the pinned test never exercises the worker path. Note git log -L845,861 shows this block was rewritten in d79f862 (2026-07-27, same wave, "route canonical artifact identities through core repository") — this is 3.2's own code, not ancient residue.

2. HIGH — vpr_id-as-application-key survives verbatim at interview_prep_handler.py:860, and the identity requirement exists only at the HTTP boundary.
:848 resolved_application_id = application_id or api_request.application_id or api_request.job_id or '' then :860 application_id=resolved_application_id or resolved_artifact_id — when identity is absent, a vpr_id is passed as application_id into get_vpr_by_artifact_id. This is the exact O-3-forbidden pattern v3.0.0 claims was "REMOVED". Reachable because the worker performs no identity guard: _generate_and_persist_from_sqs (:216) validates InterviewPrepRequest whose identity fields are typed optional, so any identity-less request_data — an old queued/DLQ-redriven message, or any direct invoke — replays pre-A1 behavior end to end. Also _request_data_from_sqs_body:176 synthesizes application_id: body.get('application_id') or job_id (for a submit-shaped body, that job_id is the job UUID, not an application id) and fabricates gap_response_ids: ['artifact-chain'] to satisfy the non-empty validator — compatibility machinery of exactly the A3 flavor the amendment rejected, living one hop downstream.

3. MEDIUM — An unguarded sibling submit path for the same route ships in two deployed lambdas.
interview_prep_handler.py:100-101 routes POST …/generate to _submit_interview_prep_request (:467). There, missing identity is NOT refused: _resolve_request_vpr_identity (:712-714) returns (None, None, None) with denial_response=None, and generation proceeds using the client vpr_id (finding 2's path). Today API Gateway wires POST /interview-prep/generate to the guarded submit handler (api_construct.py:2945, :3478), and interview_prep_handler.lambda_handler backs only the worker (:2137) and status (:3021) lambdas — so it's unreachable via the deployed route map. But it is live, untested, contract-violating code in production lambdas; one route rewire silently reverts v3.0.0 with no failing test.

4. MEDIUM — application_id != job_id mismatch is silently resolved, never detected.
Probe: application_id='app-A', job_id='job-B' → resolver received 'app-A', 202-path proceeds. Scope-lock §3 item 1 and the YAML v3.0.0 note itself say "application_id == job_id becomes mandatory, not defaulted" — yet no code anywhere checks equality; a mismatched pair writes/queries under app-A while the record's job_id field says job-B (submit handler stores full request_data at :173). The spec's Fix Plan item 4 ("Keep public application_id == job_id … stable") is unenforced.

5. LOW — str(None) → literal 'None' as an application key. interview_prep_handler.py:990: application_id = str(api_request.application_id or ctx['job_id']).strip() — when both are None (worker path, legacy message), the LLM usage context is bound to application_id='None' (the string). Corrupts cost attribution silently.

6. LOW — the refused (identity-less) request body is still fully logged. interview_prep_submit_handler.py:102 logs the entire API Gateway event (api_gateway_event=event, includes auth headers) and :117 the raw parsed body at INFO before the 400. Not introduced by a485293 (diff untouched those lines), but the new refusal path inherits full-payload logging of requests the contract now deems malformed.

7. LOW — spec wording vs pinned test on "absent". Spec RED description (spec:62) says "with both application and job ids absent"; the pinned test (test_dh4_p01_canonical_artifact.py:353-375) sends them present-null ('application_id': None, 'job_id': None keys in the JSON). Behavior is identical under the current model, but this project explicitly treats null-vs-absent as load-bearing (AC-DH4-2); true omission of both keys is unpinned for interview-prep.

def mark_requested_artifact_pending(application_id: str, user_id: str, artifact_type: str) -> None:
    app_repo = build_application_repo()
    if app_repo is None:
        return
    try:
        app_repo.update_artifact_status(
            application_id=application_id,
            user_id=user_id,
            artifact_type=artifact_type,
            status='pending',
        )
    except Exception:
        return

Round-2 adversarial review — scope-lock v3.0.0 (D-H4/P-01, decision A1)
A note on sources before anything else: round 1's report was never committed to the repository. I recovered its full text from the prior session transcript (~/.claude/projects/.../8ef79473-....jsonl, final assistant message, model claude-opus-5, 2026-07-31T11:19Z) and saved a copy to my session scratchpad. Goal A was therefore performed against round 1's actual words, not a reconstruction. The human should commit that report somewhere durable — right now the only review of record lives in a temp directory.

1. Verdict
CONFIRM-WITH-CONDITIONS — round 1's verdict survives, but its condition set does not survive as written. Two of round 1's five conditions would write errors into the record if executed verbatim, its headline semver rationale is overstated, and it missed the most important fact about this amendment: the pattern it claims was removed is still running, one hop downstream, in code written by the same wave.

REVERT was weighed seriously and fails on merits: the submit-boundary tightening is behaviorally correct (refusal fires before any resolver read or write — proven by running the real handler), no test was weakened anywhere, and reverting would restore the defect at the boundary while fixing nothing downstream. DOWNGRADE fails because MAJOR is right — though on grounds neither the row nor round 1 states (§3 below).

2. Audit of round 1
Refuted:

"The backend change is not deployed... reverting would have been cheap" — refuted. At the time round 1 ran, .github/workflows/db-redesign-checks.yml (as it existed at commit a485293 — STACK_NAME: CareerVpCrudDev, deploy job gated only on lint/mypy/unit) had auto-deployed both v3.0.0 backend commits to CareerVpCrudDev, and repo documentation (docs/frontend/spec-amplify/environment.md:27, P-26 amendment :57-59) consistently records that stack's API (4xe2tdq8z6...) as the one the deployed frontend calls. The tightening was live on the API the shipped frontend uses. Round 1's verdict didn't hinge on this, but the human's revert-cost calculus does, and round 1 pre-weighed it wrong.
"The spec's arithmetic is wrong: 7 affected, not 8" (condition C3) — refuted. The spec's inventory (D-H4-P-01-canonical-artifact-spec.md:67-109) names exactly eight tests. Round 1 counted only the two A1 commits and missed the eighth — the B1 CV-tailoring fixture in 0ef8971. Executing C3 as written ("correct 8 to 7, or 9 with C2") would put a wrong number into the twins. What is wrong in the spec is one sentence: "the other seven were found only by running the full suite... all seven are the same class" — only six fit that description; the eighth is a different class found via the B1 amendment. Round 1's "6 found late" was correct; its total was not.
"No curl examples in docs carrying bodies" — refuted in one instance. docs/refactor/EXECUTION_RUNBOOK.md:3020 documents an identity-less interview-prep curl with "Expected: 202 Accepted". Documentation, not a shipped caller — the safe-for-shipped-callers conclusion stands — but the absolute claim was false.
Overstated:

"MAJOR survives only on §3 item 10; the row as written does not support MAJOR" — overstated in both directions. The facts verified perfectly: the new 400 body {error, status_code, code} fails contract/schemas/ErrorResponse.json validation ("'code', 'status_code' were unexpected"), the frontend's .strict() zod schema rejects it, and client.ts:105 never reads code. But git show a485293^ shows every pre-existing error path in this same handler already emitted the non-conforming {error, status_code, ...} family — the handler was never item-10 conformant, so "A1 broke item 10" is unfair as stated. The sharper true statement: A1 pinned a pre-existing nonconformance via the RED test's exact-dict assertion — while pinning a fully conformant 403 envelope (error/classification/error_code/field) in the same file, proving the authors could produce conformant envelopes and chose not to. Meanwhile the row supports MAJOR better than round 1 allowed: §3's preamble second sentence — "Internal PK is free to change; the wire contract is not" (project-scope-lock.md:91) — sits inside a section headed IMMUTABLE, and A1 changed the wire contract (bodies previously answered 202 now get 400) without taking the preamble's only escape hatch, versioning the route. MAJOR lands on the ladder's first prong; the row's own "written public promise revoked" is a plain-language rendering of that. C5 (fix or formally accept the envelope deviation) survives; the rationale-substitution instruction does not.
The "either-or rule is not a per-field constraint" attribution. Round 1 called this "the amendment's stated reason" for skipping a model-layer check. The phrase exists only in the api_models.py:505-507 comment and the ae18e30 commit message — never in the proposal, the twins, or the spec, and no document in the paper trail ever weighs a model_validator alternative. Round 1's substantive point (the alternative was never considered) is upheld; its sourcing was loose.
C4's count. The O-3 misattribution is hard-coded in nine to ten written locations (both twins' P-01 clause rows, both change-log blocks, the proposal itself twice, the round-1 handoff, plus round 1's five), not five. C4 executed as scoped would leave live instances behind.

§9.3 clear, no test weakened — upheld. Every reconciliation diff is an additive fixture key; B1's change moved a fixture onto the contracted vpr_id: null shape. The pinned RED test has exactly one commit (133ced7) in its history. The 16 targeted tests pass fresh (16 passed, 2 warnings in 1.20s).

The RED test pinned the contract mechanism (exact three-key 400, request_id forbidden by comment) before any review ran; the conformant 403 sitting beside it in the same test shows the envelope was a choice the test made, not a constraint. What round 1 missed: everything in §3 below, plus the deployment reality above. Its per-attack structure followed the round-1 handoff's four attacks faithfully — and the worker path, the sibling handler, and the B1 approval gap all live outside those four.

3. Round-2 findings (new)
F1 — The pattern the amendment says was "REMOVED" survives verbatim in the async worker, written by the same wave. interview_prep_handler.py:848 reads resolved_application_id = application_id or api_request.application_id or api_request.job_id or '' and :849 resolved_artifact_id = vpr_artifact_id or api_request.vpr_id; at :860 the call passes application_id=resolved_application_id or resolved_artifact_id — a client-supplied vpr_id standing in for the canonical application key, the exact O-3/D-H9-family pattern v3.0.0's row says was removed. The worker calls _generate_interview_prep_result with neither an application id nor a resolved artifact id (:239-242), so the client vpr_id from the queued request_data drives VPR resolution on every 202'd request. git log -L shows this block was rewritten in d79f862 (2026-07-27, "route canonical artifact identities through core repository") — this is 3.2's own code, not ancient residue. Consequence: a request with a valid application_id but a wrong vpr_id passes the submit gate (which checks by application) and then reliably fails async (:879) because the worker keys on the client value the gate never validated. The amendment's identity requirement exists only at the HTTP boundary; identity-less SQS payloads (DLQ redrives, direct invokes) replay pre-A1 behavior end to end, and _request_data_from_sqs_body:176-178 even synthesizes application_id from job_id and fabricates gap_response_ids: ['artifact-chain'] — compatibility machinery of exactly the A3 flavor the amendment rejected.

F2 — A dormant, unguarded sibling submit path for the same route ships in deployed lambdas. interview_prep_handler.py:100-101 routes POST .../generate to _submit_interview_prep_request, whose identity resolution (:712-714) returns (None, None, None) with no denial when both ids are missing. Today the API Gateway route maps to the guarded submit handler (api_construct.py:234 → :2945 → route at :3478, confirmed this session), so it is unreachable — but one route rewire silently reverts v3.0.0 with no failing test.

F3 — application_id == job_id is asserted, never enforced. The YAML P-01 note says the identity "becomes mandatory, not defaulted," yet a probe with application_id='app-A', job_id='job-B' proceeds silently under app-A; no equality check exists anywhere. §3 item 1's "one identifier, two names" is unpinned for the mismatch case.

F4 — B1 rode into v3.0.0 without its own approval trail. Its amendment file declares requires adversarial review? Yes; commit 0ef8971 carries no Scope-Lock-Approved-By trailer and no quoted decision token (A1 has "A1 accepted"; B1 has only the assertion "Human-approved"). The "no contract change" label is defensible against the written contract but conflates written-contract stability with observed behavior: the endpoint's response to omitted vpr_id changed 202→400.

F5 — Residual incoherences. InterviewPrepRequest.vpr_id is still required (min_length=1, api_models.py:500) even though the submit handler never reads it as an identifier — the withdrawn-promise model mandates a field the guarded path ignores. The frontend type requires application_id specifically while the wire contract accepts job_id alone, making the documented alias type-illegal for TS callers. Three verbatim MD/YAML twin drifts exist in the v3.0.0 clause annotations (the YAML P-01 parenthetical, the D-H4 v2.7.0 sentence, "very"/"same"), and both twins describe the pinned 400 envelope with two keys where the test pins three. Empty-string and whitespace identifiers correctly 400, but the whitespace case is saved by str_strip_whitespace=True on the base model, which nothing pins.

4. Strongest surviving objection to my own conclusion
If the amendment's headline rationale — "a client-supplied vpr_id may never stand in for the canonical application key" — is still false in the running system (F1), then confirming the amendment ratifies a record that misdescribes reality, and the §9.3-honest outcome is REVERT or a re-issued amendment, not conditions. This is the best argument against CONFIRM-WITH-CONDITIONS, and it fails narrowly: the amendment's five-point operative decision (require identity on submit, remove the :144 fallback, refuse before resolution, make the contracts explicit, keep the pinned test) was implemented exactly and is verified by passing pinned tests; the surviving pattern is in code the amendment never claimed to change, introduced by a different commit (d79f862) that predates the amendment's landing. What is false is the breadth of the rationale as recorded, and the record is exactly what conditions repair. Reverting would reintroduce the boundary defect while leaving the worker untouched — strictly worse on the amendment's own terms.
